<p>
NIST subscription sites provide data under the
<a href="https://www.nist.gov/srd/" title="Link to NIST
Standard Reference Data web site">NIST Standard Reference
Data Program</a>, but require an annual fee to access.
The purpose of the fee is to recover costs associated
with the development of data collections included in
such sites. Your institution may already be a subscriber.
Follow the links above to find out more about the data
in these sites and their terms of usage.

<h3>Gas Phase Heat Capacity (Shomate Equation)</h3>
<p>
C<sub>p</sub>&deg; = A + B*t + C*t<sup>2</sup> + D*t<sup>3</sup> +
 E/t<sup>2</sup><br />
H&deg; &#8722; H&deg;<sub>298.15</sub>= A*t + B*t<sup>2</sup>/2 +
 C*t<sup>3</sup>/3 + D*t<sup>4</sup>/4 &#8722; E/t + F &#8722; H<br />
S&deg; = A*ln(t) + B*t + C*t<sup>2</sup>/2 + D*t<sup>3</sup>/3 &#8722;
 E/(2*t<sup>2</sup>) + G<br />
&nbsp; &nbsp; C<sub>p</sub> = heat capacity (J/mol*K)<br />
&nbsp; &nbsp; H&deg; = standard enthalpy (kJ/mol)<br />
&nbsp; &nbsp; S&deg; = standard entropy (J/mol*K)<br />
&nbsp; &nbsp; t = temperature (K) / 1000.<br />
</p>
<p>
<a href="/cgi/cbook.cgi?ID=C7726956&amp;Mask=FFFF&amp;Type=JANAFG&amp;Plot=on#JANAFG">View plot</a>
Requires a JavaScript / HTML 5 canvas capable browser.
</p>
<p>
<a href="/cgi/cbook.cgi?ID=C7726956&amp;Mask=FFFF&amp;Type=JANAFG&amp;Table=on#JANAFG">View table</a>.
</p>

<h3>Antoine Equation Parameters</h3>
<p>
log<sub>10</sub>(P) = A &#8722; (B / (T + C))<br />
&nbsp; &nbsp; P = vapor pressure (bar)<br />
&nbsp; &nbsp; T = temperature (K)<br />
</p>

Requires a JavaScript / HTML 5 canvas capable browser.
</p>

<h3>Notes</h3>

<table role="presentation" aria-label="List of Notes">
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia1">1</a></td><td>The interval of 3141 cm<sup>-1</sup> between X<sub>1</sub> <sup>2</sup>&#928;<sub>3/2</sub> and X<sub>2</sub> <sup>2</sup>&#928;<sub>1/2</sub> of
Br<sub>2</sub><sup>+</sup> derived from the Rydberg series does not agree with the value 2820 cm<sup>-1</sup> from
the photoelectron spectrum  <a href="#ref-57">Cornford, Frost, et al., 1971</a>.
The discrepancy may be accounted for by assuming that instead of v'=0 as suggested
in  <a href="#ref-68">Venkateswarlu, 1969</a> the <sup>2</sup>&#928;<sub>1/2</sub> series listed here have v'=2 while the <sup>2</sup>&#928;<sub>3/2</sub> series
have v'=1 (see <a href="#Dia2">2</a>).</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia2">2</a></td><td>According to the photoionization and photoelectron value of the ionization
potential (see <a href="#Dia26">26</a>) the five <sup>2</sup>&#928;<sub>3/2</sub> Rydberg series in the table refer to v'=l.
Vibrational structure; see  <a href="#ref-68">Venkateswarlu, 1969</a>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia3">3</a></td><td>Normal isotopic mixture.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia4">4</a></td><td>Extensive system of absorption bands in the region 59000 - 67000 cm<sup>-1</sup>;
no analysis. This system may include transitions to the upper states of:</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia5">5</a></td><td>a) emission systems of  <a href="#ref-69">Haranath and Rao, 1958</a></td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia6">6</a></td><td>b) a long resonance series (63817 - 53779 cm<sup>-1</sup>).</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia7">7</a></td><td>The vibrational analysis is doubtful since only v&quot;=21-32 were observed.
v<sub>00</sub> (extrapolated) and T<sub>e</sub> are different from  <a href="#ref-71">Verma, 1958</a> to allow for the new data on
the B state  <a href="#ref-82">Barrow, Clark, et al., 1974</a>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia8">8</a></td><td>System H-X of  <a href="#ref-69">Haranath and Rao, 1958</a>, not observed in absorption.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia9">9</a></td><td>&#969;<sub>e</sub>z<sub>e</sub>= +0.00006<sub>5</sub>; vibrational constants from the reanalysis  <a href="#ref-73">Wieland, Tellinghuisen, et al., 1972</a> of the emission
data of  <a href="#ref-72">Venkateswarlu and Verma, 1958</a> and the absorption data of  <a href="#ref-86">Briggs and Norrish, 1963</a>. See <a href="#Dia10">10</a> .</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia10">10</a></td><td>It is not entirely certain that the lower state is B <sup>3</sup>&#928;<sub>u,0+</sub> and not A <sup>3</sup>&#928;<sub>u,1</sub>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia11">11</a></td><td>Several absorption continua beyond 19580 cm<sup>-1</sup> corresponding to a number of
electronic transitions including that to C <sup>1</sup>&#928;<sub>u</sub> with maximum at 24000 cm<sup>-1</sup>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia12">12</a></td><td>Also observed in magnetic circular dichroism  <a href="#ref-87">Brith, Rowe, et al., 1975</a> and photofragment  <a href="#ref-88">Oldman, Sander, et al., 1975</a> spectra.
The latter authors confirm Mulliken's  <a href="#ref-77">Mulliken, 1940</a> prediction that C <sup>1</sup>&#928;<sub>u</sub> dissociates into
<sup>2</sup>&#928;<sub>3/2</sub> + <sup>2</sup>&#928;<sub>3/2</sub> and observe evidence for several excited g states by
two-photon photofragment studies near 28000 and 38000 cm<sup>-1</sup>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia13">13</a></td><td>(for v&#8804;8). Vibrational levels observed to v=55, dissociation limit
(<sup>2</sup>P<sub>3/2</sub>+<sup>2</sup>P<sub>1/2</sub>) at 19579.76 cm<sup>-1</sup> above X <sup>1</sup>&#931;<sub>g</sub><sup>+</sup>(v=0,J=0).
See <a href="#Dia16">16</a>. Absorption in the B 0<sub>u</sub><sup>+</sup> continuum  <a href="#ref-89">Bondybey, Bearder, et al., 1976</a>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia14">14</a></td><td>Hfs observed in v=12 (<sup>81</sup>Br<sub>2</sub>) and v=17(<sup>79</sup>Br<sub>2</sub>); see  <a href="#ref-90">Eng and LaTourrette, 1974</a>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia15">15</a></td><td>Predissociation was observed  <a href="#ref-91">Lum and McAfee, 1975</a>, <a href="#ref-92">Lum and Hozack, 1975</a> for v=42, J=33 by the laser-molecular beam technique.
B &#8594; X emitted in the recombination of Br(<sup>2</sup>P<sub>3/2</sub>) atoms shows strong enhancement
of bands with 5&lt;v'&lt; 10 presumably on account of inverse predissociation  <a href="#ref-93">Clyne, Coxon, et al., 1971</a>. See also <a href="#Dia18">18</a>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia16">16</a></td><td>RKR potential function and Franck-Condon factors  <a href="#ref-94">Coxon, 1971</a>, <a href="#ref-82">Barrow, Clark, et al., 1974</a>.
For the behavior of the potential function near the dissociation limit <a href="#Dia13">13</a> see  <a href="#ref-95">Goscinski, 1972</a>, <a href="#ref-96">Yee and Stone, 1973</a>, <a href="#ref-97">LeRoy, 1974</a>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia17">17</a></td><td>D<sub>v</sub> and higher order constants in  <a href="#ref-82">Barrow, Clark, et al., 1974</a>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia18">18</a></td><td>Estimated radiative lifetimes for A and B range from 1000 to 2000 and 12 to 70 &#956;s,
respectively  <a href="#ref-85">Coxon, 1972</a>, <a href="#ref-80">Coxon, 1973</a>, <a href="#ref-89">Bondybey, Bearder, et al., 1976</a>. For the B state  <a href="#ref-98">Capelle, Sakurai, et al., 1971</a> find total lifetimes of the order of 1 &#956;s;
minima (~0.2 &#956;s) occur for v=1 and 14 probably on account of predissociation.
For lifetimes near the dissociation limit <a href="#Dia13">13</a> of B see  <a href="#ref-99">McAfee and Hozack, 1976</a>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia19">19</a></td><td>Convergence limit for <sup>79</sup>Br<sub>2</sub> at 15894.6 cm<sup>-1</sup> above X <sup>1</sup>&#931;<sub>g</sub><sup>+</sup> (v=0,J=0),
corresponding to <sup>2</sup>P<sub>3/2</sub> + <sup>2</sup>P<sub>3/2</sub>.
A weak continuous spectrum joins onto the limit and overlaps the main absorption
system B &#8592; X; see  <a href="#ref-100">Sulzmann, Bien, et al., 1967</a>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia20">20</a></td><td>Extrapolated from v=7; constants for v=0.. .6 have not been determined.
B<sub>v</sub>, D<sub>v</sub>, H<sub>v</sub>, and &#923;-type doubling constants for v=7...24 in  <a href="#ref-85">Coxon, 1972</a>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia21">21</a></td><td>RKR potential function and Franck-Condon factors  <a href="#ref-85">Coxon, 1972</a>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia22">22</a></td><td>Based on &#916;G'(v=0-7) from low-resolution emission spectra  <a href="#ref-101">Clyne and Coxon, 1967</a> of normal Br<sub>2</sub>
and the origin of the 7-0 <sup>79</sup>Br<sub>2</sub> band at 14739.14 cm<sup>-1</sup> derived from  <a href="#ref-85">Coxon, 1972</a> and  <a href="#ref-82">Barrow, Clark, et al., 1974</a>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia23">23</a></td><td>RKR potential curve  <a href="#ref-94">Coxon, 1971</a>, <a href="#ref-82">Barrow, Clark, et al., 1974</a>. Hfs observed  <a href="#ref-90">Eng and LaTourrette, 1974</a> in v=4 (<sup>81</sup>Br<sub>2</sub>) and v=7 (<sup>79</sup>Br<sub>2</sub>).</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia24">24</a></td><td>Raman sp. <a href="#Dia29">29</a></td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia25">25</a></td><td>From  <a href="#ref-82">Barrow, Clark, et al., 1974</a>; corresponding values for <sup>79</sup>,<sup>81</sup>Br<sub>2</sub> and <sup>81</sup>Br<sub>2</sub> are
1.9708<sub>2</sub> and 1.9709<sub>5</sub> eV (short extrapolation of B 0<sub>u</sub><sup>+</sup>)</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia26">26</a></td><td>From photoionization  <a href="#ref-58">Dibeler, Walker, et al., 1970</a>; supported by measurements at different temperatures.
In good agreement with 10.51 eV obtained by photoelectron spectroscopy  <a href="#ref-102">Frost, McDowell, et al., 1967</a>, <a href="#ref-57">Cornford, Frost, et al., 1971</a>, <a href="#ref-56">Potts and Price, 1971</a>.
A slightly higher value, 10.56 eV, was derived  <a href="#ref-68">Venkateswarlu, 1969</a> from the Rydberg series in the VUV.
It is probable that this value refers to v'=1.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia27">27</a></td><td>System J-X of  <a href="#ref-69">Haranath and Rao, 1958</a>, not observed in absorption.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia28">28</a></td><td>(valid for v&#8804;8).</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia29">29</a></td><td>Resonance Raman spectra in the gas  <a href="#ref-103">Holzer, Murphy, et al., 1970, 2</a>, <a href="#ref-104">Baierl and Kiefer, 1975</a>, in solid argon  <a href="#ref-83">Ault, Howard, et al., 1975</a>;
pure rotational Raman spectrum  <a href="#ref-105">Baierl, Hochenbleicher, et al., 1975</a>.</td></tr>
</table>
